For the collective estimate use the global same-shell unary row,
not a global fixed-box hypothesis.  Let $\alpha_{\rm row}>0$ be the
weighted cube sublevel exponent on its local interaction box.
The two scalar bad sets in that proof and the same clipped correlation
give the uniform exponent
\[
 \beta_{\mathrm{eq},\rho}
 =\min\{\sigma\alpha_{\rm row},c_{\rm eq,\rho}^*/2\}>0.
\]
Simultaneous translation preserves the highest mixed block of the
full original phase, and dilation gives its norm
$L_R=R^m\norm{P_m}_{\rm coeff}$.  The exact Gram factor $R^{-n}$
absorbs into the normalized weights.  Supported first-moment selection
and Hilbert integration then give the actual same-shell energy
\[
 \|F_R\|_2^2\le
 C_\rho B^2\|\Theta\|_\infty^2\Lambda M_R
       (L_R^{-\beta_{\mathrm{eq},\rho}}
                         +2^{-\beta_{\mathrm{eq},\rho}s}).
\]

The separated row uses the original choice $\tau_R=L_R^{-1/2}$.
The finite-$\rho$ unequal correlation and original one-sided incidence
give a positive row exponent, uniformly over the finitely many possible
highest mixed degrees.  The two geometric sums of
$L_R^{-\beta}$ and $(2^{-s}R/S)^\beta$ give a complete far-row exponent
$\gamma_{\kappa,\rho}>0$.  This estimate is transferred to the
arbitrary singular small-shell measures, then integrated against the
large-shell total variations; its right side is
$C_{\rho,\kappa}B^2\|\Theta\|_\infty^2\Lambda
M_S2^{-\gamma_{\kappa,\rho}s}$.
All constants precede $P,m$ and the packet data, and the radial power
is still two, independent of $\kappa$.

Choose
\begin{equation}\label{eq:allq-collective-explicit-choice}
 c_{\kappa,\rho}
 =\min\{\beta_{\mathrm{eq},\rho}\min\{1,\kappa\},
                              \gamma_{\kappa,\rho}\}>0.
\end{equation}
The high threshold gives the diagonal decay with this exponent.
The fixed-width neighboring-scale Cauchy--Schwarz bound and the
oriented far Gram rows then give
\eqref{eq:allq-collective-high}, exactly as in the final Hilbert assembly
of Proposition~\ref{prop:collective-high}, without a scale-count loss.
The same proof yields $B^2$ in both conclusions, so one may take
$C_{B,\rho}=2$ (or any larger fixed finite power).  Complex linearity
identifies the literal grouped shell operators, and every estimate uses
only $|e_Q|\le1$.  No probability-space Jensen step, density assumption,
regularization of the measures, or supplied Gram bound is needed.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Terminal packets with a finite-variation radial profile}

\begin{lemma}[Smooth fixed-sign packet kernel]
\label{lem:allq-smooth-fixed-sign-packet}
Put $N_0=4n+1$ and $\eta=(16N_0)^{-1}$.  In the unit-scale packet
setting of Proposition~\ref{prop:terminalpacket}, let $c_t$ be supported in
one fixed annular enlargement and satisfy
\[
 |c_t|\le1,
 \qquad
 \|\partial_r^\ell c_t\|_\infty\le C_\ell2^{\eta\ell t}
 \quad(0\le\ell\le N_0).
\]
For arbitrary fixed packet coefficients of modulus at most one, let $S_t$
be the convolution output with kernel $K_\Theta(z)c_t(|z|)$.  Then
\begin{equation}\label{eq:allq-smooth-fixed-sign-packet}
 \|S_t\|_2^2
 \le C_n\|\Theta\|_\infty^2
       2^{-3t/32}\Lambda M.
\end{equation}
The assertion is unchanged by the input and output modulations allowed in
Proposition~\ref{prop:terminalpacket}.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
This is the smooth part of the proof of
Proposition~\ref{prop:terminalpacket}, but we record precisely which
hypotheses are used.  The fixed annular enlargement is $[1/4,4]$,
as in the full-kernel convention, and the implicit constants may depend
on the prescribed structural constants $C_\ell$.  Only radial
$C^{N_0}$ regularity is used.  Initially take a finite packet family and a
pointwise bounded angular representative as in
Proposition~\ref{prop:terminalpacket}.  Put
$H_t(z)=K_\Theta(z)c_t(|z|)$.  Its normalized radial source budget is
at most
\[
 M_{N_0}\le C\|\Theta\|_\infty2^{\eta N_0t}
 =C\|\Theta\|_\infty2^{t/16}.
\]
In dimension one, the cancellation argument in the proof of
Lemma~\ref{lem:fullkernel} gives squared energy at most
$C M_{N_0}^2 2^{-t/2}\Lambda M$.  Consequently
\[
 \|S_t\|_2^2
 \le C\|\Theta\|_\infty^2 2^{-3t/8}\Lambda M,
\]
which is stronger than \eqref{eq:allq-smooth-fixed-sign-packet}.
We may therefore assume $n\ge2$.  The one-shell kernel has normalized radial
$C^{N_0}$ size at most
$C\|\Theta\|_\infty2^{\eta N_0t}$.  Seeger's decomposition with
$\kappa=1/2$ and $\epsilon=1/4$ therefore gives a microlocal part with
squared $L^2$ norm
\[
 C\|\Theta\|_\infty^2
 2^{2\eta N_0t}2^{-t/2}\Lambda M
 =C\|\Theta\|_\infty^2 2^{-3t/8}\Lambda M
\]
and a remainder with $L^1$ norm
\[
 C\|\Theta\|_\infty
 2^{-(1/4-\eta N_0)t}M
 =C\|\Theta\|_\infty2^{-3t/16}M.
\]
The source budget enters quadratically in the microlocal energy above
and linearly in the remainder bound.  Independently, the support and
the bound $|c_t|\le1$ give, by full-annulus packing, the simultaneous
$L^\infty$ bound
$C\Lambda\|\Theta\|_\infty$.  The bounded-output estimate \eqref{eq:fixedsign} gives squared $L^2$
decay $2^{-3t/16}$, which is stronger than the required $2^{-3t/32}$;
this proves \eqref{eq:allq-smooth-fixed-sign-packet} for finite families.
For an arbitrary disjoint cube family, its positive interiors imply
countability.  If $M<\infty$, the bounded compactly supported kernel
makes the finite convolution sums converge pointwise, and Fatou gives
the same energy bound.  Infinite total mass is immediate when the
right-hand coefficient is positive; zero angular norm or zero density
gives zero output separately.  Angular representative equality follows
for every convolution point by polar coordinates and the map $y\mapsto x-y$.
No property of a step function, or of the set on which an unsmoothing error
is supported, enters these three estimates.  The proof uses absolute
packet coefficients and packet cancellation, so the modulation statements
are preserved.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Radially weighted selector-free terminal packet]
\label{prop:allq-terminal-packet}
Fix $1\le\rho<\infty$ and put
\begin{equation}\label{eq:allq-terminal-parameters}
 N_0=4n+1,
 \qquad \eta=\frac1{16N_0},
 \qquad \gamma=\frac\eta2,
 \qquad c_{\rm sm}=\frac3{32}.
\end{equation}
There is $t_{0,\rho}\in\mathbb N$ such that
Proposition~\ref{prop:terminalpacket} remains valid for $t\ge t_{0,\rho}$
after inserting a radial factor $a(|x-y|/R)$ satisfying
$\|a\|_\infty+V^\rho(a;[1,2])\le1$. More precisely, one may take
\begin{equation}\label{eq:allq-terminal-explicit-decay}
 \delta_{T,\rho}
 :=\frac14\min\left\{\gamma,c_{\rm sm},\frac\eta\rho\right\}>0
\end{equation}
so that, with the same packet hypotheses and $R\le\varepsilon\le2R$,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:allq-terminal-packet}
 \|V^2(F_\varepsilon:R\le\varepsilon\le2R)\|_2^2
 \le C_{n,\rho}\|\Theta\|_\infty^2
 2^{-\delta_{T,\rho}t}\Lambda M.
\end{equation}
The modulation invariances in Proposition~\ref{prop:terminalpacket}
remain valid.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
We first retain the full scope of the local radial hypothesis.  For
every $r\in[1,2]$, comparison with a point where $|a|$ is arbitrarily
close to its essential supremum gives
\[
 |a(r)|\le\|a\|_{L^\infty([1,2])}+V^\rho(a;[1,2])\le1.
\]
This includes both endpoints and any exceptional point values.  Extend
$a$ constantly outside $[1,2]$ by the monotone clamp
$r\mapsto\min(2,\max(1,r))$, and divide that extension by two.
The resulting profile $b$ has global pointwise
$\|b\|_\infty+V^\rho(b)\le1$ and equals $a/2$ on $[1,2]$.
It suffices to prove the estimate for $b$ and restore the factor four
in squared energy.  This normalization imposes no extra hypothesis
on $a$ and changes none of its values in the original integral.
As in Proposition~\ref{prop:terminalpacket}, a pointwise bounded angular
representative preserves every literal annular integral for every
spatial point and every radius.  We initially work with that
representative, finitely many packets, and $R=1$.

Put $k=\lceil\rho\rceil$, $J=N_0k$, and choose the binary grid
\[
 L_t=\left\lfloor\frac{t}{40J}\right\rfloor,
 \qquad K_t=2^{L_t},\qquad
 h=(8K_t^2)^{-k}.
\]
Let $I_\ell=(1+(\ell-1)/K_t,1+\ell/K_t]$ and let $G_\ell$
be the full shell output over $I_\ell$ with radial profile $b$.
Inserting all cell endpoints into an arbitrary variation partition
gives the deterministic decomposition
\[
 V^2(F_\varepsilon:1\le\varepsilon\le2)
 \le C V^2\left(\sum_{\ell\le m}G_\ell:0\le m\le K_t\right)
 +C\left(\sum_\ell V^2(F_\varepsilon:\varepsilon\in\overline I_\ell)^2\right)^{1/2}.
\]
The within-cell argument uses $V^2\le V^1$, the positive thin-layer
majorant, and only $|b|\le1$.  The layer thickness is
$K_t^{-1}+C_n2^{-t}$; since $2^{-t}\le K_t^{-1}$, it gives
\begin{equation}\label{eq:allq-terminal-within-cell}
 \left\|\left(\sum_\ell
 V^2(F_\varepsilon:\varepsilon\in\overline I_\ell)^2\right)^{1/2}\right\|_2^2
 \le C_n\|\Theta\|_\infty^2\Lambda M K_t^{-1}.
\end{equation}

For the grid partial sums fix signs
$\epsilon_\ell\in\{-1,0,1\}$ and set
\[
 c(r)=b(r)\sum_{\ell=1}^{K_t}\epsilon_\ell\mathbf1_{I_\ell}(r).
\]
The literal half-open step factor has supremum at most one and
$\rho$-variation at most $2K_t^{1/\rho}$.  The product rule gives
\begin{equation}\label{eq:allq-signed-profile}
 \|c\|_\infty\le1,
 \qquad V^\rho(c)\le1+2K_t^{1/\rho},
 \qquad \supp c\subset[1,2].
\end{equation}
Mollify this zero-extended profile by a nonnegative smooth unit-mass
kernel at scale $h$.  Lemma~\ref{lem:allq-translation} yields
\begin{equation}\label{eq:allq-terminal-smooth-error-L1}
 \|c-c_h\|_1
 \le C_\rho h^{1/\rho}(1+K_t^{1/\rho})
 \le C_\rho K_t^{-1}.
\end{equation}
Indeed $k\ge\rho\ge1$ implies
$h^{1/\rho}\le(8K_t^2)^{-1}$ and $K_t^{1/\rho}\le K_t$.
Both profiles are bounded by one and supported in a fixed annular
enlargement, since $h\le1/8$.  Their difference need not be supported
near the grid endpoints: the radial profile may vary throughout every
cell.  For its actual convolution output $D$, full-annulus packing
and the polar-coordinate kernel $L^1$ bound give, respectively,
\begin{align}
 \|D\|_\infty
 &\le C_n\Lambda\|\Theta\|_\infty,
 \label{eq:allq-terminal-unsmooth-Linf}\\
 \|D\|_1
 &\le C_n\|\Theta\|_\infty M\|c-c_h\|_1
 \le C_{n,\rho}\|\Theta\|_\infty M K_t^{-1}.
 \label{eq:allq-terminal-unsmooth-L1}
\end{align}
The packing estimate uses only the pointwise kernel bound, the fixed
annular support, disjoint packet interiors, and
$\|d_U\|_1\le\Lambda|U|$; no small $L^\infty$ error is asserted.
Consequently
\begin{equation}\label{eq:allq-terminal-unsmooth-L2}
 \|D\|_2^2
 \le C_{n,\rho}\|\Theta\|_\infty^2\Lambda M K_t^{-1}.
\end{equation}

For the smoothed signed sum $S$, positivity of the mollifier gives
$|c_h|\le1$ and
$\|\partial_r^\ell c_h\|_\infty\le C_\ell h^{-\ell}$ with no
additional factor $K_t$.  Thus the actual one-shell kernel
$K_\Theta(z)c_h(|z|)$ has radial source budget at most
$C_n\|\Theta\|_\infty h^{-N_0}$.  The proof of
Lemma~\ref{lem:fullkernel} explicitly establishes the stronger decay
$2^{-t/8}$ in every positive dimension before weakening it to its
stated exponent.  Applying that proved bound at this single scale gives
\begin{equation}\label{eq:allq-terminal-smoothed-L2}
 \|S\|_2^2
 \le C_n\|\Theta\|_\infty^2\Lambda M h^{-2N_0}2^{-t/8}
 \le C_{n,\rho}\|\Theta\|_\infty^2\Lambda M K_t^{-1}.
\end{equation}
For the last inequality, $L_t\le t/(40J)$ implies
\[
 h^{-2N_0}2^{-t/8}
 =8^{2J}2^{4JL_t-t/8}
 \le8^{2J}2^{-t/40}\le8^{2J}K_t^{-1}.
\]
This argument does not require the different smoothing scale in
Lemma~\ref{lem:allq-smooth-fixed-sign-packet}.

The fixed-sign bound obtained from
\eqref{eq:allq-terminal-unsmooth-L2} and
\eqref{eq:allq-terminal-smoothed-L2}, followed by
Lemma~\ref{lem:RM}, costs at most $C(1+L_t)^2$ in squared energy.
Absorb just one sixteenth of the binary-depth exponent:
\[
 (1+L_t)^2 2^{-L_t}
 \le C2^{-15L_t/16}
 \le 2C2^{-3t/(128N_0k)}.
\]
Together with \eqref{eq:allq-terminal-within-cell} and the deterministic
decomposition, this proves the desired continuum estimate with decay
$3/(128N_0k)$.  The displayed exponent in the statement is exactly
\[
 \delta_{T,\rho}
 =\min\left\{\frac1{128N_0},\frac1{64N_0\rho}\right\}
 \le\frac3{128N_0\lceil\rho\rceil}.
\]
For $1\le\rho\le2$ use $\lceil\rho\rceil\le2$; for $\rho\ge2$
use $2\lceil\rho\rceil\le3\rho$.  Hence no reduction of the
original displayed decay is needed.  Choose $t_{0,\rho}$ large enough
for the fixed packet-diameter and full-kernel thresholds.

The physical change of variables $x=Rx'$, $y=Ry'$ proves the result
for every $R>0$: use packets $d_U(R\,\cdot)$ on the dilated cubes,
whose total mass is $R^{-n}M$, and cancel this factor against the
output Jacobian $R^n$.  The normalized radial profile is unchanged.
Disjoint positive cube interiors make any original cube family
countable.  If $M<\infty$, finite packet sums converge in $L^1$; the
bounded clipped annular kernels give path convergence uniformly in
$R\le\varepsilon\le2R$ at every $x$.  Lower semicontinuity on each
finite variation partition and Fatou's lemma extend the estimate to
the full family.  If $M=\infty$ and
$\Lambda\|\Theta\|_\infty>0$, the extended-valued bound is immediate.
The zero-density and zero-angular-norm cases instead have identically
zero output and are treated before this multiplication, so no ambiguous
zero-times-infinity argument is used.  Restoring $a=2b$ on $[1,2]$
only multiplies the energy constant by four.  Finally packetwise input
modulations preserve all absolute-value hypotheses and use precisely
the stipulated cancellation of the modulated packets; common output
modulation preserves every variation increment.  This proves all the
claimed invariances and \eqref{eq:allq-terminal-packet}.
\end{proof}

\subsection{The unphased strong theorem for every finite radial exponent}

We next prove the terminal strong input in a form with the exact angular
conjugate-exponent loss needed for the $L\log L$ endpoint.

For $j\in\mathbb Z$ define the annular measure
\begin{equation}\label{eq:allq-shell-measure}
 d\nu_j(x)=
 \frac{\Omega(x/|x|)}{|x|^n}b(|x|)
 \mathbf1_{\{2^j<|x|\le2^{j+1}\}}\,dx.
\end{equation}
The mean-zero condition on $\Omega$ implies $\nu_j(\R^n)=0$.

\begin{lemma}[Shell Fourier envelope]
\label{lem:allq-shell-Fourier}
Assume \eqref{eq:allq-normalize-b} and
$\int_{\Sph^{n-1}}\Omega\,d\sigma=0$.  There exists
$\beta_\rho>0$ such that $\widehat\nu_j(0)=0$ and, for
$t=2^j|\xi|>0$,
\begin{align}
 |\widehat\nu_j(\xi)|
 &\le C t\|\Omega\|_1,
 &&0<t\le1,
 \label{eq:allq-shell-low}\\
 |\widehat\nu_j(\xi)|
 &\le C_\rho t^{-\beta_\rho}\|\Omega\|_2,
 &&t\ge1.
 \label{eq:allq-shell-high-L2}
\end{align}
One may take
\[
 \beta_\rho=\min\left\{\frac14,\frac1{2\rho}\right\}.
\]
Consequently, if $1<s\le2$, $s'=s/(s-1)$, and $\Omega$ has mean zero,
then, for $t>0$,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:allq-shell-Ls}
 |\widehat\nu_j(\xi)|
 \le C_\rho\|\Omega\|_s\,a_{s,\rho}(\log_2t),
\end{equation}
where $a_{s,\rho}:\mathbb R\to(0,\infty)$ is defined by
\begin{equation}\label{eq:allq-envelope}
 a_{s,\rho}(r)=
 \begin{cases}
 C2^r,&r\le0,\\
 C_\rho2^{-2\beta_\rho r/s'},&r>0.
 \end{cases}
\end{equation}
For every $\delta>0$,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:allq-envelope-sum}
 \sum_{r\in\mathbb Z}a_{s,\rho}(r)^\delta
 \le C_{\rho,\delta}s'.
\end{equation}
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Use the angular Fourier convention $\widehat f(\xi)=\int
f(z)e^{-iz\cdot\xi}\,dz$.  In the convention with exponential
$e^{-2\pi iz\cdot\eta}$ the same transform is evaluated at
$\eta=\xi/(2\pi)$; this conversion does not change the shell or its
radial representative.  Polar coordinates and $r=2^ju$ give, for
$\xi\ne0$, $e=\xi/|\xi|$ and $t=2^j|\xi|$,
\[
 \widehat\nu_j(\xi)=\int_{\Sph^{n-1}}\Omega(\theta)
 H_{j,t}(e\cdot\theta)\,d\sigma(\theta),\qquad
 H_{j,t}(\tau)=\int_{(1,2]}b(2^ju)e^{-itu\tau}\frac{du}{u}.
\]
These are integrals of the literal shell density on
$(2^j,2^{j+1}]$; the radial normalization ensures their
integrability.  At zero frequency the radial integral is independent
of $\theta$, giving $\widehat\nu_j(0)=0$.  Subtract this value before
using $|e^{-iz}-1|\le|z|$.  Since $\|b\|_\infty\le1$, the result is
$|\widehat\nu_j(\xi)|\le t\|\Omega\|_1$, without evaluating a logarithm
at zero.

Lemma~\ref{lem:allq-calculus} bounds the variation of
$u\mapsto b(2^ju)/u$ on the fixed interval uniformly in $j$.
The finite-variation first-derivative estimate of
Lemma~\ref{lem:allq-first-derivative} therefore gives
\[
 |H_{j,t}(\tau)|\le C_\rho,
 \qquad |H_{j,t}(\tau)|\le C_\rho(t|\tau|)^{-1/\rho}
 \quad(\tau\ne0).
\]
Here and below the constants are fixed before $j,b,s,\Omega,\xi$.
For $t\ge1$ split the sphere into the strip
$F_t=\{\theta:|e\cdot\theta|<t^{-1/2}\}$ and its complement.
The spherical strip bound is
$\sigma(F_t)\le C_n t^{-1/2}$; in dimension one the narrow strip is
empty, so the same estimate is valid.  This uses no assertion about
a density of the spherical projection in that dimension.
For $1<s\le2$ put $a=1-1/s=1/s'$.  H\"older on the strip and the
radial bound off it yield directly
\[
 |\widehat\nu_j(\xi)|
 \le C_{n,\rho}\|\Omega\|_s
       \bigl(t^{-a/2}+t^{-1/(2\rho)}\bigr)
 \le C_{n,\rho}\|\Omega\|_s t^{-2\beta_\rho/s'}.
\]
The last inequality follows from $0<a\le1/2$,
$\beta_\rho\le1/4$ and $\beta_\rho\le1/(2\rho)$.
The finite-measure factors are uniform in $s$, since
$z^a\le\max(1,z)$ for $0\le a\le1$.  At $s=2$ this is exactly
\eqref{eq:allq-shell-high-L2}; together with the low estimate and
$L^s\hookrightarrow L^1$ on the sphere it proves
\eqref{eq:allq-shell-Ls} with the stated asymmetric envelope.

For completeness, the nonpositive integer part of the envelope sum is
bounded by $C^\delta/(1-2^{-\delta})$.  The positive part is bounded by
\[
 C_\rho^\delta\sum_{r=1}^{\infty}
 e^{-(2\beta_\rho\delta\log2)r/s'}
 =\frac{C_\rho^\delta}{e^{(2\beta_\rho\delta\log2)/s'}-1}
 \le\frac{C_\rho^\delta s'}{2\beta_\rho\delta\log2}.
\]
This proves both convergence and \eqref{eq:allq-envelope-sum}, with
one linear factor $s'$ and no hidden $s$-dependence.
\end{proof}

We shall also use the following standard vector-valued shell estimate.  A
proof is included because it is uniform down to angular $L^1$ and therefore
is exactly what allows spatial interpolation below.

\begin{lemma}[Uniform vector-valued annular estimate]
\label{lem:allq-vector-shell}
For $1<p<\infty$ and arbitrary functions $f_j$,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:allq-vector-shell}
 \left\|\left(\sum_j|\nu_j*f_j|^2\right)^{1/2}\right\|_p
 \le C_p\|b\|_\infty\|\Omega\|_1
 \left\|\left(\sum_j|f_j|^2\right)^{1/2}\right\|_p.
\end{equation}
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Let $B=\|b\|_\infty$, interpreted as an essential radial norm, and let
$M_\theta^+g(x)=\sup_{a>0}a^{-1}\int_0^a|g(x-r\theta)|\,dr$.
For each shell, polar coordinates and $r^{-1}\le2^{-j}$ on
$(2^j,2^{j+1}]$ give, almost everywhere,
\[
 |\nu_j*f_j(x)|
 \le 2B\int_{\Sph^{n-1}}|\Omega(\theta)|
             M_\theta^+f_j(x)\,d\sigma(\theta).
\]
Only the absolute inputs $|f_j|$ enter this domination.  The essential
radial inequality holds inside the radial integral; changing $b$ on
a radial null set leaves the kernel unchanged almost everywhere.
The same applies to the original angular and input representatives.

For a finite shell set, Minkowski in $\ell^2$ and then in $L^p$ gives
\[
 \left\|\{\nu_j*f_j\}_j\right\|_{L^p(\ell^2)}
 \le2B\int_{\Sph^{n-1}}|\Omega(\theta)|
        \left\|\{M_\theta^+f_j\}_j\right\|_{L^p(\ell^2)}
        \,d\sigma(\theta).
\]
The one-dimensional vector maximal theorem, lifted along the orthogonal
fibers $x=x_\perp+r\theta$, bounds the inner norm by
$C_p\|\{f_j\}_j\|_{L^p(\ell^2)}$.  The coordinate change preserves
Lebesgue measure, so $C_p$ is independent of both direction and ambient
dimension.  Angular integration contributes exactly $\|\Omega\|_1$;
no normalized-sphere factor, variation budget, or angular supremum norm
is introduced.  In particular this is a bound for the vector of
maximal functions, not for a scalar maximal function of the vector norm.

Increasing finite shell sets proves the countable inequality by
monotone convergence.  Equivalently, countable integral Minkowski
follows here by Tonelli and finite-dimensional Cauchy--Schwarz followed
by exhaustion.  If the input square has finite $L^p$ norm, every
coordinate is in $L^p$; the $L^1$ shell kernel then gives genuine
almost-everywhere convolution integrability.  These integrability and
representative identities hold on one conull set for all integer
coordinates.  If the input norm is infinite and $B\|\Omega\|_1>0$,
the extended-norm assertion is immediate.  If either factor is zero,
the literal shell kernels vanish almost everywhere and so do all their
convolutions.  This also covers the zero-times-infinite case.
\end{proof}

Use the explicit cutoffs $\psi,\widetilde\psi$ from
\eqref{eq:dual-cutoff-reproduction}. Let $\Delta_\ell$ and
$\widetilde\Delta_\ell$ have multipliers $\psi(2^{-\ell}\xi)$ and
$\widetilde\psi(2^{-\ell}\xi)$. Thus
$\widetilde\Delta_\ell\Delta_\ell=\Delta_\ell$ and
$\sum_\ell\widetilde\Delta_\ell\Delta_\ell f=f$ in $L^p$ for
$1<p<\infty$. The square-function bounds below therefore apply to
$\widetilde\Delta_\ell\Delta_\ell f=\Delta_\ell f$; no identity
$\sum_\ell\psi(2^{-\ell}\xi)^2=1$ is assumed. For a relative frequency
$r\in\mathbb Z$ put
\[
 g_{j,r}:=\nu_j*\widetilde\Delta_{r-j}\Delta_{r-j}f.
\]
On the Fourier support of $g_{j,r}$ one has $2^j|\xi|\simeq2^r$.

\begin{lemma}[Frequency-separated tail representation]
\label{lem:allq-separated-tail}
For every $1<p<\infty$ and every $f\in L^p(\R^n)$, there is a fixed
integer $M\ge5$ such that, after splitting the shell
indices $j$ into their $M$ residue classes, the tail
\[
 \sum_{\substack{j\ge k\\j\equiv c\ ({\rm mod}\ M)}}g_{j,r}
\]
is, up to repetitions as $k$ varies, exactly a subsequence of the smooth
low-pass family $\{\phi_\ell*h_{r,c}\}_{\ell\in\mathbb Z}$ of
Lemma~\ref{radialmod:smooth-jump}, applied to
\[
 h_{r,c}:=\sum_{j\equiv c\ ({\rm mod}\ M)}g_{j,r}.
\]
Here $\widehat\phi=1$ on $|\xi|\le2$ and
$\widehat\phi=0$ on $|\xi|>4$, and
$\widehat{\phi_\ell}(\xi)=\widehat\phi(2^\ell\xi)$.
Consequently, Lemma~\ref{radialmod:smooth-jump} gives
\begin{equation}\label{eq:allq-tail-jump-by-h}
 \left\|\mathbf J\left(
 \sum_{\substack{j\ge k\\j\equiv c\ ({\rm mod}\ M)}}g_{j,r}:k\in\mathbb Z
 \right)\right\|_p
 \le C_p\|h_{r,c}\|_p.
\end{equation}
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
We use the angular Fourier convention $e^{-ix\cdot\xi}$.
The annular cutoff $\psi$ and low-pass kernel $\phi$ have the stated
multipliers; kernels defined using the cyclic-frequency convention
are dilated spatially by $2\pi$. Their plateaux are therefore $2,4$
and their annular radii are $1/2,2$. The dual-cutoff identity gives
$g_{j,r}=\nu_j*\widetilde\Delta_{r-j}\Delta_{r-j}f=\nu_j*\Delta_{r-j}f$.

We first construct the sums in $L^p$. The annular vector estimate
and the Littlewood--Paley square estimate imply
\[
 \left\|\left(\sum_j|g_{j,r}|^2\right)^{1/2}\right\|_p<\infty
 \quad\hbox{for every fixed }r.
\]
Choose a fixed wider smooth annular reproducer equal to one on the
support of each $\Delta_{r-j}$.  It reproduces $g_{j,r}$ as an $L^p$
identity, by convolution associativity, first on a dense class and then
by the bounded $L^1$ kernel operators.  The dual Littlewood--Paley
estimate therefore bounds every finite sum over a set $F$ by
$C_p\|(\sum_{j\in F}|g_{j,r}|^2)^{1/2}\|_p$.
The latter tends to zero for sets escaping finite windows, by dominated
convergence.  Consequently the series over $j$ converges
unconditionally in $L^p$, as do its residue subseries and their tails.

Take $M=5$.  The Fourier support of $g_{j,r}$ lies in
$2^{r-j-1}\le|\xi|\le2^{r-j+1}$.  For $k\in\mathbb Z$ let
$j_0(k)$ be the least integer at least $k$ congruent to $c$ modulo $5$.
The multiplier of $\phi_{j_0(k)-r}$ equals one on every retained
support with $j\ge j_0(k)$.  The preceding retained support starts at
$2^{r-j_0(k)+4}$, strictly beyond the low-pass support endpoint
$2^{r-j_0(k)+2}$; hence the multiplier vanishes on all earlier retained
supports.  Apply the bounded convolution operator to the unconditional
$L^p$ sum to obtain exactly
\[
 F_{r,c,k}:=\sum_{\substack{j\ge k\\j\equiv c\ (5)}}g_{j,r}
       =\phi_{j_0(k)-r}*h_{r,c}\quad\hbox{in }L^p.
\]
The map $k\mapsto j_0(k)-r$ is nondecreasing.  Repetitions cannot create
jumps, so Lemma~\ref{radialmod:smooth-jump} gives
\eqref{eq:allq-tail-jump-by-h} for representatives on a common conull
set.  Its paired-path estimate dominates $\mathbf J$; the comparison
\eqref{eq:chain-pair-functional} also supplies the chain version.

We record precisely the pointwise meaning of these series.  All shell
convolutions and all displayed low-pass convolutions are absolutely
integrable almost everywhere.  Countability of $r,c,k,j$ puts these
integrability assertions, their representative identities, and every
finite-window identity on one conull set.  Unconditional $L^p$
convergence gives $F_{r,c,k}\to0$ as $k\to+\infty$ and
$F_{r,c,k}\to h_{r,c}$ as $k\to-\infty$ in $L^p$.
The finite pointwise jump functional supplied by the preceding bound
ensures pointwise limits at both ends; subsequences of the $L^p$
limits identify them as $0$ and $h_{r,c}$ almost everywhere.
On the same conull set,
\[
 \sum_{\substack{k\le j<l\\j\equiv c\ (5)}}g_{j,r}
       =F_{r,c,k}-F_{r,c,l}.
\]
Letting $l\to+\infty$ proves convergence of every ordered tail;
letting $k=-N$, $l=N$ proves convergence of the centered ordered totals.
These conclusions hold simultaneously for all $r,c,k$.  They do not
assert unconditional or absolute convergence pointwise in space.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[$s'$-tracked dyadic terminal jump]
\label{lem:allq-terminal-dyadic}
Let $1<s\le2$, assume $\Omega\in L^s(\Sph^{n-1})$ has mean zero, and fix
$1<p<\infty$.  Then, for every $f\in L^p(\R^n)$, under
\eqref{eq:allq-normalize-b},
\begin{equation}\label{eq:allq-terminal-dyadic}
 \left\|\mathbf J(T^{0,b}_{\Omega,2^k}f:k\in\mathbb Z)\right\|_p
 \le C_{n,p,\rho}s'\|\Omega\|_s\|f\|_p.
\end{equation}
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
First impose finite shell and frequency cutoffs.  For one residue class,
Littlewood--Paley theory, Lemma~\ref{lem:allq-vector-shell}, and bounded
frequency overlap give, for every $1<p_0<\infty$,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:allq-h-uniform}
 \|h_{r,c}\|_{p_0}\le C_{p_0}\|\Omega\|_1\|f\|_{p_0}
 \le C_{p_0}\|\Omega\|_s\|f\|_{p_0}.
\end{equation}
On $L^2$, the separated Fourier supports and
Lemma~\ref{lem:allq-shell-Fourier} give the following bound.  Indeed, on
$\supp\widehat{\Delta_{r-j}f}$ one has
$|\log_2(2^j|\xi|)-r|\le1$.  The two exponential slopes in
\eqref{eq:allq-envelope} are uniformly bounded for $1<s\le2$; including
the possible crossing of $r=0$, this gives
$a_{s,\rho}(\log_2(2^j|\xi|))\le C_\rho a_{s,\rho}(r)$ with a constant
independent of $s$.  Hence
\begin{equation}\label{eq:allq-h-L2}
 \|h_{r,c}\|_2
 \le C_\rho a_{s,\rho}(r)\|\Omega\|_s\|f\|_2.
\end{equation}
If $p<2$, interpolate \eqref{eq:allq-h-L2} with
\eqref{eq:allq-h-uniform} at one fixed $p_0<p$; if $p>2$, use one fixed
$p_0>p$.  For $p=2$ use \eqref{eq:allq-h-L2}.  Thus for some
$\theta_p>0$ independent of $s,r$,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:allq-h-decay}
 \|h_{r,c}\|_p
 \le C_{p,\rho}a_{s,\rho}(r)^{\theta_p}
 \|\Omega\|_s\|f\|_p.
\end{equation}
Lemma~\ref{lem:allq-separated-tail} gives the same estimate for the paired
jump of the corresponding residue-class tail.  By
$\mathbf J^{\rm ch}\le\sqrt2\,\mathbf J$ from
\eqref{eq:chain-pair-functional}, and then the uniform equivalence in
Lemma~\ref{lem:jumpnorm}, the same bound holds in the norm
$\|\cdot\|_{\mathfrak J_D}$ for every finite ordered parameter set $D$.
Summing the finitely many residue classes changes only the constant.

We now remove the cutoffs rather than merely invoking lower
semicontinuity.  Fix a finite ordered set $D\subset\mathbb Z$ of dyadic
truncation indices and use the norm $\|\cdot\|_{\mathfrak J_D}$ supplied by
Lemma~\ref{lem:jumpnorm}.  The preceding paired-to-chain conversion is
what permits this normed summation.  If $R_2>R_1$, its triangle
inequality and \eqref{eq:allq-h-decay} give, uniformly in the finite shell
cutoff,
\[
 \left\|\sum_{R_1<|r|\le R_2}
  \biggl[\sum_{j\ge k}g_{j,r}\biggr]_{k\in D}
 \right\|_{L^p(\mathfrak J_D)}
 \le C_{p,\rho}\|\Omega\|_s\|f\|_p
 \sum_{R_1<|r|\le R_2}a_{s,\rho}(r)^{\theta_p}.
\]
By \eqref{eq:allq-envelope-sum} the right side tends to zero as
$R_1\to\infty$, so the frequency sums are Cauchy in
$L^p(\mathfrak J_D)$.  For a fixed shell $j$, the Littlewood--Paley
identity $\sum_r\widetilde\Delta_{r-j}\Delta_{r-j}f=f$ holds in $L^p$; convolution with the
finite-annulus measure $\nu_j$ identifies every finite-shell coordinate of
the limit.

Next remove the shell cutoff with the order of the two tails made
explicit.  Let $\mathcal J_J\uparrow\mathbb Z$ be the growing finite shell
sets and put $E_J=\mathbb Z\setminus\mathcal J_J$.  Given $\epsilon>0$,
first choose $R$ so large that
\[
 C_{p,\rho}\|\Omega\|_s\|f\|_p
 \sum_{|r|>R}a_{s,\rho}(r)^{\theta_p}<\frac\epsilon2;
\]
this estimate is uniform in the shell cutoff.  For the finitely many
$|r|\le R$ and residue classes $c$, put
\[
 h_{r,c}^{E_J}=\sum_{\substack{j\in E_J\\j\equiv c\ ({\rm mod}\ M)}}
      \nu_j*\widetilde\Delta_{r-j}\Delta_{r-j}f.
\]
The separated-tail estimate applies to an arbitrary subset
$E\subset\{j:j\equiv c\ ({\rm mod}\ M)\}$, not only to the full residue
class: the low-pass multiplier used in its proof still gives, term by term,
\[
 \phi_{j_0-r}*h_{r,c}^{E}
 =\sum_{\substack{j\in E,\ j\ge j_0\\j\equiv c\ ({\rm mod}\ M)}}g_{j,r}.
\]
Thus Lemma~\ref{radialmod:smooth-jump}, with the same constant, controls
the tail for every such $E$.
Interpret this sum first over finite subsets of $E_J$.  The following
uniform estimate makes those partial sums Cauchy in $L^p$ and hence
defines $h_{r,c}^{E_J}$; the finite-parameter tail estimate then passes to
that limit through the norm $\mathfrak J_D$.
The proof of \eqref{eq:allq-h-uniform}, restricted to $E_J$, gives
\[
 \|h_{r,c}^{E_J}\|_p
 \le C_p\|\Omega\|_1
 \left\|\left(\sum_{j\in E_J}
       |\widetilde\Delta_{r-j}\Delta_{r-j}f|^2\right)^{1/2}\right\|_p
 \longrightarrow0.
\]
Indeed, for fixed $r$ the map $j\mapsto r-j$ is a bijection of
$\mathbb Z$, and
\[
 \left(\sum_{j\in E_J}|\widetilde\Delta_{r-j}\Delta_{r-j}f|^2\right)^{1/2}
 \longrightarrow0\quad\hbox{pointwise},\qquad
 \left(\sum_{j\in E_J}|\widetilde\Delta_{r-j}\Delta_{r-j}f|^2\right)^{1/2}
 \le \left(\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb Z}|\widetilde\Delta_\ell\Delta_\ell f|^2\right)^{1/2}.
\]
The dominating full Littlewood--Paley square function belongs to $L^p$;
dominated convergence proves the asserted norm limit.  This also shows
that the limit is independent of the particular exhaustion
$\mathcal J_J$.
Choose $J$ so that the sum of these finitely many norms is less than
$\epsilon/2$.  Lemma~\ref{lem:allq-separated-tail}, the triangle
inequality in $L^p(\mathfrak J_D)$, and the preceding frequency-tail
choice then show that the full omitted contribution has
$L^p(\mathfrak J_D)$ norm below $\epsilon$.  This proves joint Cauchy
convergence, independently of the order in which the two cutoffs are
removed.

To retain the constant coordinate, fix $k_0\in D$. For each residue
$c$, the exact low-pass representation in
Lemma~\ref{lem:allq-separated-tail} writes $F_{r,c,k_0}$ as a convolution
of $h_{r,c}$ with a kernel of fixed $L^1$ norm. Thus
\eqref{eq:allq-h-decay} and the summability of
$a_{s,\rho}(r)^{\theta_p}$ show that
$\sum_{r,c}F_{r,c,k_0}$ converges absolutely in $L^p$.
The omitted-shell estimate proved above applies to this representation
as well, so the finite shell sums converge to the same coordinate.
The Littlewood--Paley identity identifies it with the original tail
on bounded compactly supported inputs. For general $L^p$ inputs,
the local absolute-tail estimate of Section~\ref{mod:atom:sec:tails}
and finite-annulus exhaustion give the same identification. Choose
this $L^p$ limit as the $k_0$-coordinate of the quotient representative.
Finite-dimensional quotient convergence
controls every difference between a $k$-coordinate and the
$k_0$-coordinate; the same coordinatewise Littlewood--Paley identity
therefore identifies all coordinates with
$T^{0,b}_{\Omega,2^k}f$.  Convergence in $L^p(\mathfrak J_D)$ gives, after
a subsequence, convergence in the finite-dimensional quotient almost
everywhere.  The equivalence in Lemma~\ref{lem:jumpnorm} first returns the
resulting bound to $\mathbf J^{\rm ch}_D$; the inequality
$\mathbf J_D\le2\mathbf J^{\rm ch}_D$ in
\eqref{eq:chain-pair-functional}, or equivalently finite strict-witness
lower semicontinuity, then gives the paired estimate
\[
 \|\mathbf J(T^{0,b}_{\Omega,2^k}f:k\in D)\|_p
 \le C_{p,\rho}\sum_r a_{s,\rho}(r)^{\theta_p}
 \|\Omega\|_s\|f\|_p
 \le C_{p,\rho}s'\|\Omega\|_s\|f\|_p.
\]
Finally exhaust $D$. Lemma~\ref{radialmod:smooth-jump} estimates the
paired norm and hence the paired jump count. The paired-to-chain
comparison is used only
to enter the explicit finite-path quotient norm for summation, and the chain-to-paired
comparison is used on exit.  Both losses are absolute and independent of
$D$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[$s'$-tracked short terminal variation]
\label{lem:allq-terminal-short}
Under the hypotheses of Lemma~\ref{lem:allq-terminal-dyadic}, and for
every $f\in L^p(\R^n)$, let
$S_2(T^{0,b}_\Omega f)$ denote the square sum of the within-dyadic-shell
$2$-variations. Then
\begin{equation}\label{eq:allq-terminal-short}
 \|S_2(T^{0,b}_\Omega f)\|_p
 \le C_{n,p,\rho}s'\|\Omega\|_s\|f\|_p.
\end{equation}
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
We first estimate the literal partial-shell paths
\[
 A_{j,u}f(x)=\int_{u2^j<|z|\le2^{j+1}}
 b(|z|)K_\Omega(z)f(x-z)\,dz,\qquad 1\le u\le2.
\]
These vanish at $u=2$.  Their kernels are genuinely in $L^1$ under
angular $L^1$ and the normalized radial bound; no
angular supremum bound is used.  Put
\[
 S_rf=\left(\sum_{j\in\mathbb Z}
 V^2(A_{j,u}\widetilde\Delta_{r-j}\Delta_{r-j}f:1\le u\le2)^2
 \right)^{1/2}.
\]
The dual cutoff is a reproducer, so the input is exactly
$\widetilde\Delta_{r-j}\Delta_{r-j}f=\Delta_{r-j}f$.

For almost every $x$, full-shell absolute integrability makes the path
absolutely continuous, with actual derivative
\[
 \partial_u A_{j,u}g(x)
 =-\frac{b(2^ju)}u\int_{\Sph^{n-1}}\Omega(\theta)
                            g(x-2^ju\theta)\,d\sigma(\theta)
\]
for almost every $u$.  Integrating its absolute value bounds $V^2$ by
$C\|b\|_\infty\int|\Omega(\theta)|M_\theta g(x)\,d\sigma(\theta)$.
The countable vector maximal estimate used in
Lemma~\ref{lem:allq-vector-shell} and the Littlewood--Paley square
bound give, for every $1<p_0<\infty$,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:allq-short-uniform}
 \|S_rf\|_{p_0}\le C_{n,p_0}\|\Omega\|_1\|f\|_{p_0}
 \le C_{n,p_0}\|\Omega\|_s\|f\|_{p_0}.
\end{equation}
The constants are fixed before $r,s,\Omega,b,f$.

To obtain decay, apply the finite-variation radial estimate on the
actual interval $(u,2]$, uniformly for $1\le u\le2$.
The zero-extension of $v\mapsto b(2^jv)/v$ on this interval has
uniformly bounded $\rho$-variation, including the two endpoint
increments. Mean-zero angular cancellation gives
the low-frequency factor $2^j|\xi|$; the direct strip--H\"older
argument of Lemma~\ref{lem:allq-shell-Fourier} gives the high-frequency
factor $(2^j|\xi|)^{-2\beta_\rho/s'}$.
On the localized frequency annulus this proves
\begin{equation}\label{eq:allq-partial-shell-envelope}
 \sup_{1\le u\le2}|m_{j,u}(\xi)|
 \le C_{n,\rho}\|\Omega\|_s a_{s,\rho}(r).
\end{equation}
The angular/cyclic Fourier conversion is $\xi=2\pi\eta$;
its fixed scale shift is absorbed into $C_{n,\rho}$, uniformly in $s$.

For a localized input $g$, Plancherel and the preceding uniform
multiplier estimate bound the joint path norm in $L^2(dx\,du)$ by
$C_{n,\rho}\|\Omega\|_s a_{s,\rho}(r)\|g\|_2$.
The actual derivative has joint $L^2$ norm at most
$C_n\|b\|_\infty\|\Omega\|_1\|g\|_2$, by translation invariance
and Minkowski.  Fubini and the fundamental theorem of calculus
justify these as the path and its derivative, not merely formal
multiplier derivatives.  Since $A_{j,2}g=0$, the anchored
Sobolev--variation inequality gives
\[
 \|V^2(A_{j,u}g:1\le u\le2)\|_2
 \le C_{n,\rho}a_{s,\rho}(r)^{1/4}\|\Omega\|_s\|g\|_2.
\]
Squaring, summing over every integer $j$, and applying the localized
$L^2$ square estimate proves
\begin{equation}\label{eq:allq-short-L2}
 \|S_rf\|_2\le C_{n,\rho}a_{s,\rho}(r)^{1/4}
                         \|\Omega\|_s\|f\|_2.
\end{equation}
Thus the $L^2$ assertion concerns the genuine continuum $V^2$ square
inside the spatial norm, not a supremum of separate operator norms.

The square of dense-parameter variations is a measurable sublinear
operator on every $L^{p_0}$: use the variation triangle inequality,
countable-vector Minkowski, and the actual convolution domain above.
Interpolate this operator between \eqref{eq:allq-short-uniform} and
\eqref{eq:allq-short-L2}.  Choose the second spatial exponent using
only the target $p$.  For some $\vartheta_p>0$ this gives
\[
 \|S_rf\|_p\le C_{n,p,\rho}a_{s,\rho}(r)^{\vartheta_p}
                         \|\Omega\|_s\|f\|_p.
\]
One may normalize by $\|\Omega\|_s$ when it is positive; when it is
zero the original kernels vanish almost everywhere.  Consequently the
angular factor stays to exactly the first power, and neither the
interpolation exponent nor its constant depends on $s$.
Lemma~\ref{lem:allq-shell-Fourier} now gives
\[
 \sum_{r\in\mathbb Z}\|S_rf\|_p
 \le C_{n,p,\rho}s'\|\Omega\|_s\|f\|_p.
\]

Here the reconstruction is at the level of the actual paths.
Finite Littlewood--Paley sums converge to $f$ in $L^p$; convolution
with each fixed $L^1$ partial-shell kernel preserves that limit.
The preceding summable bounds give absolute summability of the path
coordinates almost everywhere for each $j$ and each dense endpoint,
since a coordinate is bounded by its variation anchored at $u=2$.
Thus the coordinate sums agree with $A_{j,u}f$, simultaneously for
all integer $j$ and dense endpoints on one conull set.
The variation triangle inequality on finite partitions, countable
Minkowski, and exhaustion yield
\[
 \left(\sum_jV^2(A_{j,u}f:1\le u\le2)^2\right)^{1/2}
 \le\sum_rS_rf\quad\hbox{almost everywhere}.
\]
Full-shell integrability makes each original path continuous, so the
dense-endpoint variations here equal the continuum variations.
This proves the required bound for the partial-shell square.

Finally identify that square with the original global zero-phase
truncation.  There is one conull set on which every positive-cutoff
tail is absolutely integrable.  To see this, prove integrability for
countably many cutoffs using the positive radial tail and angular
$L^1$ integration; domination by a smaller rational cutoff then gives
all positive cutoffs.  On that same set, for every integer $j$ and
every $u\in[1,2]$,
\[
 T^{0,b}_{\Omega,2^ju}f(x)
 =A_{j,u}f(x)+T^{0,b}_{\Omega,2^{j+1}}f(x).
\]
Subtracting at any two real endpoints cancels the identical outer
tail.  The increasing parametrization $u\mapsto2^ju$ is onto the
whole dyadic shell; hence it preserves its $V^2$ exactly.
Taking the all-integer square therefore identifies, almost everywhere,
the partial-shell square with $S_2(T^{0,b}_\Omega f)$, not just with a
majorant or a chosen finite-window trajectory.  The displayed sum
bound proves \eqref{eq:allq-terminal-short} with one factor $s'$ and
$C_{n,p,\rho}$ chosen before $s$.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Terminal $s'$-tracked critical jump]
\label{prop:allq-terminal-sp}
For $1<s\le2$, mean-zero $\Omega\in L^s(\Sph^{n-1})$, and
$1<p<\infty$, the following holds for every $f\in L^p(\R^n)$:
\begin{equation}\label{eq:allq-terminal-sp}
 \|\mathbf J(T^{0,b}_{\Omega,\varepsilon}f:\varepsilon>0)\|_p
 \le C_{n,p,\rho}s'\|\Omega\|_s\|f\|_p
\end{equation}
under normalization \eqref{eq:allq-normalize-b}.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The deterministic long--short inequality
\eqref{eq:pointwise-longshort} gives pointwise
\[
 \mathbf J(T^{0,b}_{\Omega,\varepsilon}f)
 \le C\left[
 S_2(T^{0,b}_\Omega f)+
 \mathbf J(T^{0,b}_{\Omega,2^k}f:k\in\mathbb Z)
 \right].
\]
Apply Lemmas~\ref{lem:allq-terminal-dyadic}
and~\ref{lem:allq-terminal-short}.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Unphased terminal $L\log L$ theorem]
\label{prop:allq-terminal-LlogL}
For mean-zero $\Omega\in L\log L(\Sph^{n-1})$, $1<p<\infty$, and every
$f\in L^p(\R^n)$,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:allq-terminal-LlogL}
 \|\mathbf J(T^{0,b}_{\Omega,\varepsilon}f:\varepsilon>0)\|_p
 \le C_{n,p,\rho}\mathfrak L(\Omega)\|f\|_p
\end{equation}
under \eqref{eq:allq-normalize-b}.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
We use exactly the finite-trajectory Orlicz extrapolation used in the proof
of Proposition~\ref{prop:strong-far-endpoint}, but record the cancellation
issue.
Let
\[
 \Pi\Theta=\Theta-\sigma(\Sph^{n-1})^{-1}\int_{\Sph^{n-1}}\Theta\,d\sigma.
\]
For a finite ordered truncation set $D$, let $E_D$ be the scalar
finite-path quotient endowed with a norm uniformly equivalent to the
pointwise critical jump functional.  Define the linear map
\[
 \mathcal A_{f,D}\Theta
 =\bigl[T^{0,b}_{\Pi\Theta,\varepsilon}f\bigr]_{\varepsilon\in D}
 \in L^p(\R^n;E_D).
\]
Since $\Pi$ is bounded on every $L^s$ on the finite-measure sphere,
Proposition~\ref{prop:allq-terminal-sp} gives uniformly in $D$
\[
 \|\mathcal A_{f,D}\|_{L^s(\Sph^{n-1})\to L^p(E_D)}
 \le C_{p,\rho}s'\|f\|_p,
 \qquad1<s\le2.
\]
Pair with a norm-one functional in $[L^p(E_D)]^*$ and take
$s=k/(k-1)$ for integers $k\ge2$.  For each $k$ the resulting scalar
functional on the sphere is represented by $h_k\in L^k$ satisfying
$\|h_k\|_k\le C_{p,\rho}k\|f\|_p$.  These representatives agree almost
everywhere: two of them represent the same functional on bounded angular
functions, their difference lies in $L^1$ on the finite-measure sphere,
and testing against its bounded complex sign makes that difference zero.
Denote the common representative by $h$.  If $\|f\|_p>0$, Tonelli and the
exponential series give, for a sufficiently large structural $L$,
\[
 \int_{\Sph^{n-1}}\Psi_0\left(\frac{|h|}{L\|f\|_p}\right)d\sigma
 \le\sum_{k=2}^\infty\frac{(C_{p,\rho}k/L)^k}{k!}\le1.
\]
The case $f=0$ is immediate.  Hence
\[
 \|h\|_{\exp L}\le C_{p,\rho}\|f\|_p.
\]
Orlicz--H\"older therefore yields
\[
 \|\mathcal A_{f,D}\Omega\|_{L^p(E_D)}
 \le C_{p,\rho}\mathfrak L(\Omega)\|f\|_p
\]
for mean-zero $\Omega$, uniformly in $D$.  Increase finite rational sets and
use finite-annulus local absolute continuity to recover all positive
parameters.  This proves \eqref{eq:allq-terminal-LlogL}.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Strong polynomial phases}

\begin{proposition}[Radially weighted annular decay]
\label{prop:allq-annular-decay}
Let $P$ be a real polynomial on $\R^n\times\R^n$ of total degree at most
$d$, and let $\Theta\in L^\infty(\Sph^{n-1})$.  For $R>0$ and
$1/2\le u\le1$ put
\[
 A^{P,\Theta,b}_{R,u}f(x)=
 \int_{uR<|x-y|<R}e^{iP(x,y)}b(|x-y|)
 K_\Theta(x-y)f(y)\,dy.
\]
Under \eqref{eq:allq-normalize-b}, for every nonterminal flag position
$\mu$ there are $a_{\mu,\rho}>0$ and $C_{n,d,\rho}$ such that, if the
first nonzero flag coordinate of $P$ is $\pm1$ at position $\mu$, then
\begin{equation}\label{eq:allq-annular-decay}
 \sup_{1/2\le u\le1}\|A^{P,\Theta,b}_{R,u}\|_{2\to2}
 \le C_{n,d,\rho}R^{-a_{\mu,\rho}}\|\Theta\|_\infty,
 \qquad R\ge1.
\end{equation}
Every later phase coefficient remains unrestricted.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
We revisit the three branches in Proposition~\ref{prop:annulardecay}.

For a transverse pivot, the kernel entering $A_Q^*A_Q$ is obtained from
the original $g_h(v)$ by multiplication by
$\overline{b(|v|)}b(|v-h|)$. The compact polynomial operator is applied
to this function only through its $L^2$ norm. Since
$\|b\|_\infty\le1$, the bound \eqref{eq:gh-L2}, the polynomial negative
moment in $h$, and both Hilbert--Schmidt square roots are unchanged.
Thus the original transverse exponent remains valid.

For a skew pivot, the twisted-convolution argument uses only the $L^2$
size of the annular kernel. Inserting $b$ decreases that size by at most
$\|b\|_\infty$; the original $R^{-1}$ decay is unchanged.

For a convolution pivot, write the first nonzero homogeneous convolution
term as $Q_s$, with
$\|Q_s\|_{\mathrm{coeff}}\ge c_{n,d}$. After the change $r=Rv$, the
one-dimensional fibre amplitude is
\[
 v\longmapsto b(Rv)v^{-1}\mathbf1_{[u,1]}(v).
\]
Its zero extension has uniformly bounded $L^\infty+V^\rho$ norm by
Lemma~\ref{lem:allq-calculus}. Let
$c_{\mathrm{poly},\rho}>0$ be the exponent in
Corollary~\ref{cor:allq-polynomial-vdc} for degrees at most $d$. Plancherel
on each line therefore gives the fibre bound
\[
 C_\rho\min\{1,(R^s|Q_s(\theta)|)^{-c_{\mathrm{poly},\rho}}\}.
\]
Choose
\begin{equation}\label{eq:allq-convolution-annular-beta}
 \beta_{s,\rho}
 :=\frac12\min\left\{c_{\mathrm{poly},\rho},\frac1s\right\}>0.
\end{equation}
The last display is bounded by
$C_\rho R^{-s\beta_{s,\rho}}|Q_s(\theta)|^{-\beta_{s,\rho}}$, and
\eqref{eq:negsphere} makes the angular integral finite uniformly in all
lower coefficients. Thus the convolution branch has exponent
$s\beta_{s,\rho}>0$.

Take $a_{\mu,\rho}$ to be the corresponding branch exponent. There are
only finitely many flag positions, so all constants are structural and
coefficient-uniform.
\end{proof}

For test functions, polar coordinates show that
$u\mapsto A^{P,\Theta,b}_{R,u}f$ is an absolutely continuous
$L^2$-valued path even when $b$ has jumps.  For almost every $u$,
\[
 \partial_uA^{P,\Theta,b}_{R,u}f(x)
 =-u^{-1}b(uR)\int_{\Sph^{n-1}}\Theta(\theta)
 e^{iP(x,x-uR\theta)}f(x-uR\theta)\,d\sigma(\theta).
\]
Consequently
\[
 \sup_{1/2\le u\le1}
 \|\partial_uA^{P,\Theta,b}_{R,u}f\|_q
 \le C\|b\|_\infty\|\Theta\|_1\|f\|_q,
 \qquad1\le q\le\infty.
\]
The anchored Sobolev--variation argument of
Lemma~\ref{lem:shortannular} therefore gives
\begin{equation}\label{eq:allq-short-annular-decay}
 \|V^2(A^{P,\Theta,b}_{R,u}f:1/2\le u\le1)\|_2
 \le C_{n,d,\rho}R^{-a_{\mu,\rho}/4}
 \|\Theta\|_\infty\|f\|_2.
\end{equation}
Put
\begin{equation}\label{eq:allq-global-annular-exponent}
 a_{0,\rho}
 :=\min\left(\left\{\frac{a_{\mu,\rho}}4:
                 \mu\ {\rm nonterminal}\right\}\cup\{1\}\right)>0.
\end{equation}
The added value $1$ is used only when there is no nonterminal position.

\begin{lemma}[Weighted $q'$-tracked partial-annulus estimates]
\label{lem:allq-qtracked-annulus}
Let $P$ be normalized and nonterminal, $R\ge1$, $1<q\le2$, and
$q'=q/(q-1)$.  Let $\Theta\in L^q(\Sph^{n-1})$.  Under
\eqref{eq:allq-normalize-b}, for every $1<p<\infty$ set
\[
 \gamma_{p,\rho}:=\frac{a_{0,\rho}}{2\max(p,p')}>0.
\]
Then, for every $f\in L^p(\R^n)$,
\begin{align}
 \sup_{1/2\le u\le1}
 \|A^{P,\Theta,b}_{R,u}f\|_p
 &\le C_{n,d,p,\rho}R^{-\gamma_{p,\rho}/q'}
       \|\Theta\|_q\|f\|_p,
 \label{eq:allq-qtracked-annulus}\\
 \|V^2(A^{P,\Theta,b}_{R,u}f:1/2\le u\le1)\|_p
 &\le C_{n,d,p,\rho}R^{-\gamma_{p,\rho}/q'}
       \|\Theta\|_q\|f\|_p.
 \label{eq:allq-qtracked-short}
\end{align}
All constants are uniform in the later phase coefficients and in the
location of the variation of $b$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
For fixed $P,R,u,b$, the map
$\Theta\mapsto A^{P,\Theta,b}_{R,u}$ is linear into
$\mathcal B(L^2)$.  Its angular $L^1$ bound follows from the annular
$L^1$ kernel and $\|b\|_\infty\le1$; its angular $L^\infty$ bound is
Proposition~\ref{prop:allq-annular-decay}.  Complex interpolation, whose
$L^\infty$ weight is $1/q'$, gives, after weakening the finite minimum of
the nonterminal exponents to $a_{0,\rho}$,
\[
 \sup_u\|A^{P,\Theta,b}_{R,u}\|_{2\to2}
 \le C_{n,d,\rho}R^{-a_{0,\rho}/q'}\|\Theta\|_q.
\]
The preceding a.e. derivative estimate and the anchor
$A^{P,\Theta,b}_{R,1}=0$ give, exactly as in
\eqref{eq:strong-far-a14b34},
\[
 \|V^2(A^{P,\Theta,b}_{R,u}f:u)\|_2
 \le C_{n,d,\rho}R^{-a_{0,\rho}/(4q')}
       \|\Theta\|_q\|f\|_2.
\]
On the other hand $V^2\le V^1$ and the same derivative formula give
nondecaying strong $(1,1)$ and $(\infty,\infty)$ bounds with norm
$C\|\Theta\|_1$.  Marcinkiewicz interpolation puts weight $2/p'$ on the
$L^2$ endpoint when $p<2$ and weight $2/p$ when $p>2$.  This proves
\eqref{eq:allq-qtracked-short} with the displayed, slightly weakened,
$\gamma_{p,\rho}$.  The same spatial interpolation starting from the
fixed-$u$ $L^2$ estimate proves the stronger fixed-$u$ bound and hence
\eqref{eq:allq-qtracked-annulus}.  Approximation in $f$ and $\Theta$ is
justified by the nondecaying $V^1$ estimate.
\end{proof}

Apply this lemma to the dyadic far path.  The annular pieces and their short
variations have $L^p$ norm
$C2^{-\gamma_{p,\rho}j/q'}\|\Theta\|_q\|f\|_p$; hence their geometric sum
is at most $Cq'\|\Theta\|_q\|f\|_p$.  The deterministic dyadic
long--short decomposition therefore proves the weighted analogue of
Proposition~\ref{prop:strong-far-q}.  On every finite truncation set, pair
the resulting linear angular map with a norm-one dual functional.  Taking
$q=k/(k-1)$ gives the compatible bounds $\|h\|_k\le Ck\|f\|_p$; the
common-representative and exponential-series argument used in
Proposition~\ref{prop:allq-terminal-LlogL} yields the weighted analogue of
Proposition~\ref{prop:strong-far-endpoint}.

Finally, the local triangular reduction of
Proposition~\ref{prop:strong-local-reduction} acquires the factor
$b(|x-y|)$ in its error kernel; its absolute value is bounded by one, and
the residual later-phase path carries the same $b$. Backward induction
over the finite flag, with the unphased terminal estimate
Proposition~\ref{prop:allq-terminal-LlogL}, therefore proves
\begin{equation}\label{eq:allq-strong-core}
 \|\mathbf J(T^{P,b}_{\Omega,\varepsilon}f)\|_p
 \le C_{n,d,p,\rho}\mathfrak L(\Omega)\|f\|_p
\end{equation}
under \eqref{eq:allq-normalize-b}. The normalizing dilation sends $b$ to
$b(R\,\cdot)$, and \eqref{eq:allq-dilation-b} preserves its radial norm;
hence the estimate is uniform in every coefficient of $P$.

\subsection{Phase-adapted packets with finite radial variation}

\begin{proposition}[Radially weighted phase-adapted one-shell packet]
\label{prop:allq-direct-packet}
For every fixed $1\le\rho<\infty$,
Proposition~\ref{prop:direct-packet} remains valid with the radial factor
$b(|x-y|)$ under \eqref{eq:allq-normalize-b}. Namely, there are
$c_{P,\rho},c_{E,\rho}>0$ and $t_{0,\rho}$ such that, whenever the
packet separation parameter satisfies $t\ge t_{0,\rho}$,
\begin{align}
 \|V^2(G_\varepsilon:R\le\varepsilon\le2R)\|_2^2
 &\le C_{n,d,\rho}\|\Theta\|_\infty^2
 2^{-c_{P,\rho}t}\Lambda M,
 \label{eq:allq-direct-G}\\
 \|V^1(E_\varepsilon:R\le\varepsilon\le2R)\|_1
 &\le C_{n,d,\rho}\|\Theta\|_1
 2^{-c_{E,\rho}t}M.
 \label{eq:allq-direct-E}
\end{align}
The estimates are uniform under packetwise scalar coefficients of modulus
at most one.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
We repeat the finite-mode low/high phase-block induction in
Proposition~\ref{prop:direct-packet}, but choose all parameters after the
radial exponents have been fixed. Let
$c_{\mathrm{flat},\rho}$ and $C_{B,\rho}$ be the constants in
Corollary~\ref{cor:allq-high-shell-stability}, let
$\delta_{T,\rho}$ be given by
\eqref{eq:allq-terminal-explicit-decay}, and let $C_{\rm Four}$ dominate
all powers in Lemma~\ref{lem:block-fourier}. Decrease
$c_{\mathrm{flat},\rho}$ if necessary so that it is at most one.
Here and below $C_{B,\rho}$ has first been enlarged once to dominate every
radial-amplitude power in the equal-shell, unequal-shell, flat-block, and
collective-shell estimates; it is not enlarged after the next parameter is
chosen.  Put
\begin{equation}\label{eq:allq-packet-theta}
 \theta_\rho
 :=\frac{c_{\mathrm{flat},\rho}}
          {16(C_{B,\rho}+1)}.
\end{equation}
Choose $B_{*,\rho}>1$ so large that
\begin{equation}\label{eq:allq-packet-ratio}
 \frac{2C_{\rm Four}}{B_{*,\rho}-1}
 \le\min\{\theta_\rho/2,c_{\mathrm{flat},\rho}/8\}.
\end{equation}
Put $N_d=\max(d-1,0)$ and choose
$0<\kappa_{*,\rho}\le1$ so small that, for every possible number
$0\le N\le N_d$ of nonpure homogeneous blocks,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:allq-packet-terminal-budget}
 2C_{\rm Four}\sum_{r=1}^N
 \kappa_{*,\rho}B_{*,\rho}^{\,r-N}
 \le\min\{\delta_{T,\rho}/2,1/2\}.
\end{equation}
Set
\[
 \kappa_r=\kappa_{*,\rho}B_{*,\rho}^{\,r-N},
 \qquad S_r=\sum_{i<r}\kappa_i,
 \qquad \gamma_r=\theta_\rho\kappa_r.
\]
Then $S_r\le\kappa_r/(B_{*,\rho}-1)$ and
\begin{align}
 \gamma_r-2C_{\rm Four}S_r&\ge\gamma_r/2,
 \label{eq:allq-packet-hierarchy-a}\\
 c_{\mathrm{flat},\rho}\kappa_r
 -C_{B,\rho}\gamma_r-2C_{\rm Four}S_r
 &\ge c_{\mathrm{flat},\rho}\kappa_r/2,
 \label{eq:allq-packet-hierarchy-b}\\
 c_{\mathrm{flat},\rho}
 -C_{B,\rho}\gamma_r-2C_{\rm Four}S_r
 &\ge c_{\mathrm{flat},\rho}/2.
 \label{eq:allq-packet-hierarchy-c}
\end{align}
These are the complete compatibility conditions; the choice is
noncircular because all quantities on their right were fixed before
$B_{*,\rho}$ and $\kappa_{*,\rho}$.

Suppose block $r$ is the first high block. The preceding low blocks have
squared Fourier loss at most $2^{2C_{\rm Four}S_rt}$. Partition the
normalized shell into $K_r\simeq2^{\gamma_rt}$ cells. The within-cell
square function uses only $\|b\|_\infty\le1$ and contributes
$C2^{-\gamma_rt}\|\Theta\|_\infty^2\Lambda M$. A fixed
Rademacher--Menshov choice of cell signs produces the radial profile
\[
 b(Ru)\sum_{\ell=1}^{K_r}\epsilon_\ell\mathbf1_{I_\ell}(u).
\]
By \eqref{eq:allq-b-step}, its $L^\infty+V^\rho$ norm is at most
$C_\rho(1+K_r^{1/\rho})$. Applying
\eqref{eq:allq-flat-high-block} and Lemma~\ref{lem:RM} therefore gives,
before the preceding Fourier loss is included,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:allq-direct-high-grid}
 C_\rho t^2\|\Theta\|_\infty^2
 2^{(C_{B,\rho}/\rho)\gamma_rt}\Lambda M
 \{2^{-c_{\mathrm{flat},\rho}\kappa_rt}
   +2^{-c_{\mathrm{flat},\rho}t}\}.
\end{equation}
Since $\rho\ge1$, replace $C_{B,\rho}/\rho$ by $C_{B,\rho}$.
Equations \eqref{eq:allq-packet-hierarchy-a}--
\eqref{eq:allq-packet-hierarchy-c}, after multiplication by the squared
Fourier loss, leave the three exponents
$\gamma_r/2$, $c_{\mathrm{flat},\rho}\kappa_r/2$, and
$c_{\mathrm{flat},\rho}/2$. Thus, after absorbing the polynomial factor
$t^2$, one may take
\begin{equation}\label{eq:allq-direct-high-exponent}
 c_{r,\rho}
 :=\frac14\min\{\gamma_r,
    c_{\mathrm{flat},\rho}\kappa_r,c_{\mathrm{flat},\rho}\}>0.
\end{equation}
Every high branch therefore has $E=0$ and geometric $L^2(V^2)$ decay.

If all blocks are low, expand them all with the same period $T$.
The combined characters are $e^{i\omega_F u\cdot X}$ and
$e^{i\omega_F v\cdot Y}$. Freezing the latter at each packet centre
leaves a mean-zero packet; its remainder has the structural factor
$\omega_F|v|2^{-t}$, absorbed by the unchanged weighted Fourier sum. Thus
Proposition~\ref{prop:allq-terminal-packet} applies. The squared Fourier
loss is at most
$2^{2C_{\rm Four}\sum_r\kappa_rt}$, and
\eqref{eq:allq-packet-terminal-budget} gives, at the level of the full
squared estimate,
\[
 2^{-\delta_{T,\rho}t}
 \left(2^{C_{\rm Four}\sum_r\kappa_rt}\right)^2
 \le 2^{-\delta_{T,\rho}t/2}.
\]
The input-modulation remainder is estimated
in total truncation $1$-variation exactly as in
Proposition~\ref{prop:direct-packet}; multiplication by $b$ costs only
$\|b\|_\infty\le1$. The weighted Fourier sum and
\eqref{eq:allq-packet-terminal-budget} leave the bound
$C\|\Theta\|_1 2^{-t/2}M$.

All Fourier expansions are first truncated. If $K'>K$ and block $r$
is the first high block, define
\[
 \mathfrak B_{r,t,\rho}
 :=2^{-\gamma_rt}
 +t^2 2^{(C_{B,\rho}/\rho)\gamma_rt}
   \{2^{-c_{\mathrm{flat},\rho}\kappa_rt}
      +2^{-c_{\mathrm{flat},\rho}t}\}.
\]
Applying the within-cell and signed-grid estimates only to Fourier tuples
omitted at level $K$ gives, on every normalized interacting block pair,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:allq-direct-high-fourier-cauchy}
 \|G^{K'}-G^K\|_{L^2(V^2)}
 \le C_\rho\|\Theta\|_\infty(\Lambda\widetilde M)^{1/2}
       \mathfrak B_{r,t,\rho}^{1/2}\omega_K(\{i:i<r\}),
 \qquad E^{K'}-E^K=0.
\end{equation}
If all blocks are low, the radial terminal-packet estimate and the
freezing remainder give the following sharper tail estimates.  Here one
does \emph{not} replace the omitted Fourier coefficients by the global
majorant $2^{C_{\rm Four}\sum_r\kappa_rt}$: the exact common tail
$\omega_K$ is retained.  Consequently the terminal estimate contributes
its raw square-root factor $2^{-\delta_{T,\rho}t/2}$ before the omitted
tuples are summed:
\begin{align}
 \|G^{K'}-G^K\|_{L^2(V^2)}
 &\le C_\rho\|\Theta\|_\infty
       2^{-\delta_{T,\rho}t/2}
       (\Lambda\widetilde M)^{1/2}\omega_K(\{1,\ldots,N\}),
 \label{eq:allq-direct-low-good-cauchy}\\
 \|E^{K'}-E^K\|_{L^1(V^1)}
 &\le C\|\Theta\|_1 2^{-t}\widetilde M
       \omega_K(\{1,\ldots,N\}).
 \label{eq:allq-direct-low-error-cauchy}
\end{align}
Here $\widetilde M$ is the normalized block mass. Since
$\omega_K(S)\to0$ by \eqref{eq:direct-fourier-tail-zero}, the finite-mode
decompositions are Cauchy in the anchored $L^2(V^2)$ and $L^1(V^1)$
spaces. The absolute common Fourier majorants give uniform convergence
of the phase factors on the compact normalized blocks; dominated
convergence therefore identifies the variation-space limit with the
original trajectory. Fatou on finite parameter partitions preserves all
displayed bounds.

For the branch $N=0$ there is no high block and we assign its squared
decay exponent to be $\delta_{T,\rho}/2$.  Thus the final minimum is never
over an empty set: take $c_{P,\rho}$ to be the minimum of
$\delta_{T,\rho}/2$ and all high-branch exponents $c_{r,\rho}$ arising
for the finitely many cases $1\le N\le N_d$, and take
$c_{E,\rho}=1/2$. Increase $t_{0,\rho}$ so that it dominates the
terminal-packet threshold and absorbs the finitely many polynomial
factors used above. Rescaling and the bounded-overlap block summation are
exactly those in the final paragraph of Proposition~\ref{prop:direct-packet};
the $L^2$-square and $L^1$ Jacobians cancel the normalized mass factor.
This proves \eqref{eq:allq-direct-G}--\eqref{eq:allq-direct-E}.
\end{proof}

\paragraph{Rescaling the radial profile.}
The direct realization following Proposition~\ref{prop:direct-packet}
works with the radial factor as well.  On a physical shell of radius
$R>0$, use the actual local profile
\[
 b_R(u)=b(Ru),\qquad 1\le u\le2,
\]
not the unscaled profile $b(u)$.  Under $x=a+RX$, $y=a+RY$,
the homogeneity of the angular kernel gives the exact identity
\[
 K_\Theta(x-y)b(|x-y|)\,dy
 =K_\Theta(X-Y)b_R(|X-Y|)\,dY.
\]
The essential supremum of $b_R$ on $[1,2]$ is no greater than that of
$b$ on $(0,\infty)$: multiplication by $R$ and its inverse preserve
Lebesgue null sets.  Every increasing finite sequence in $[1,2]$
maps to an increasing finite sequence in $[R,2R]$, so its radial
$\rho$-variation is also bounded by the global variation.  Hence
\[
 \|b_R\|_{L^\infty([1,2])}+V^\rho(b_R;[1,2])
 \le \|b\|_{\mathcal V^\rho}.
\]
This is an essential-norm statement and does not replace the norm by a
pointwise supremum.  The radially weighted terminal and flat estimates
can thus be applied to $b_R$ with the same constants on every shell.
Choose their positive decay exponents first, then the finite hierarchy
of phase thresholds as in the direct realization.  The low branch uses
the same frozen integral, now including $b_R$, and its physical
remainder; the high branch uses the full literal weighted trajectory
and zero remainder.  Fourier summation commutes with these integrals
by the common absolutely summable envelope.  Finally the whole-cube
grouping and the cancelling mass Jacobians are unchanged.  This proves
\eqref{eq:allq-direct-G}--\eqref{eq:allq-direct-E} for the original
physical radial factor with no additional normalization hypothesis.


\subsection{The weak endpoint}

We first treat the unphased long path, where one additional modification is
essential.

\begin{lemma}[Whole-profile smoothing in the unphased weak long path]
\label{lem:allq-whole-profile-smoothing}
Fix a shell $2^j<|z|\le2^{j+1}$ and put
\[
 a_j(u)=b(2^ju)\mathbf1_{(1,2]}(u),
 \qquad 1/2<u<3.
\]
Under \eqref{eq:allq-normalize-b},
$\|a_j\|_\infty+V^\rho(a_j)\le C$ uniformly in $j$.  If
$a_{j,h}=a_j*\phi_h$, then
\begin{align}
 \|a_j-a_{j,h}\|_1&\le C_\rho h^{1/\rho},
 \label{eq:allq-weak-smooth-L1}\\
 \|\partial^Na_{j,h}\|_\infty&\le C_Nh^{-N}.
 \label{eq:allq-weak-smooth-derivative}
\end{align}
Consequently, in the long-variation part of the proof of Proposition~\ref{prop:nonoscweak} one
may replace the smoothing of the sharp shell indicator by smoothing the
entire profile $a_j$.  The sharp--smooth difference has total
$V^1L^1$ norm $O_\rho(h^{1/\rho}\Sigma)$, while the smoothed full-kernel
estimate has exactly the derivative hypothesis used in the proof of Lemma~\ref{lem:fullkernel}.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Extend $a_j$ by zero outside $(1,2]$ and use a nonnegative unit-mass
$C^\infty$ mollifier supported in $[-1,1]$.  The normalization
\eqref{eq:allq-normalize-b} gives $|b(r)|\le1$ at every positive radius.
Thus $\|a_j\|_\infty\le1$ and $V^\rho(a_j)\le3$, the two added
endpoint jumps accounting for the extra variation.  In particular
positive convolution gives the sharp bound $|a_{j,h}|\le1$; no
factor-two renormalization is used.

The translation estimate of Lemma~\ref{lem:allq-translation}, integrated
against the positive mollifier, gives
\[
 \|a_j-a_{j,h}\|_1
 \le4\,3^{1-1/\rho}h^{1/\rho}.
\]
Differentiating the convolution gives
$\|\partial^Na_{j,h}\|_\infty\le\|\partial^N\phi\|_1h^{-N}$.
For the small smoothing range $0<h\le1/4$ used in the long path,
$\supp a_{j,h}\subset[3/4,9/4]$.  This is a fixed enlarged annulus:
the difference need not be supported near the two shell endpoints,
nor near finitely many jump boundaries of $b$.

Let $E_j$ be the literal sharp-minus-smooth kernel with angular datum
$\omega_s$.  Polar coordinates, using $u^{-1}\le4/3$ on this enlarged
annulus, give
\[
 \|E_j\|_1\le\frac{16}{3}\,3^{1-1/\rho}
          \|\omega_s\|_1h^{1/\rho}.
\]
For a finite family, each increment of a suffix path is the sum over
one interval of indices; those intervals are disjoint in a variation
partition.  Hence
\[
 V^1\left(\sum_{j\ge k}E_j*B_{j-s}:k\right)
 \le\sum_j|E_j*B_{j-s}|.
\]
Young's inequality and the assignment of each bad cube to at most one
shell, with its fixed coefficient of modulus at most one, prove
\[
 \left\|V^1(\hbox{sharp long path}-\hbox{smooth long path})\right\|_1
 \le C_\rho\|\omega_s\|_1h^{1/\rho}
       \sum_j\|B_{j-s}\|_1
 \le C_\rho\|\omega_s\|_1h^{1/\rho}\Sigma.
\]
Finite-family exhaustion gives the same estimate when $\Sigma<\infty$;
the summable $L^1$ majorant justifies the actual suffix limits.
There is no loss depending on the number or overlap of shells.

At physical scale the smooth kernel is exactly
$H_j^s(z)=K_{\omega_s}(z)a_{j,h}(2^{-j}|z|)$.
For $r>0$, a unit direction $\theta$, and $0\le\ell\le N$, apply
Leibniz' rule to
$\omega_s(\theta)r^{-n}a_{j,h}(2^{-j}r)$.
Every nonzero term has $r\simeq2^j$ and is bounded after multiplication
by $r^{n+\ell}$ by $C_{n,N}|\omega_s(\theta)|h^{-N}$.
Therefore
\[
 \sup_{r>0,\,0\le\ell\le N}
 r^{n+\ell}|\partial_r^\ell H_j^s(r\theta)|
 \le C_{n,N}|\omega_s(\theta)|h^{-N},\qquad
 \|H_j^s\|_1\le C_n\|\omega_s\|_1.
\]
These are the required weighted ray-derivative and mass bounds for
the very same whole-profile family.  They impose no angular derivative
assumption, and give the full-kernel constant
$M_N\le C_{n,N}\|\omega_s\|_\infty h^{-N}$ used in
Lemma~\ref{lem:fullkernel}.
\end{proof}


\begin{lemma}[One-dimensional fixed-sign full-kernel estimate]
\label{lem:allq-fullkernel-1d}
Let $H_j$ be odd $C^2$ kernels on $\R$, supported where
$2^{j-2}\le|x|\le2^{j+2}$, and assume
\[
 \|H_j\|_1\le M,
 \qquad
 \|H_j'\|_\infty\le M2^{-2j},
 \qquad
 \|H_j''\|_\infty\le M2^{-3j}.
\]
Let $\mathcal Q$ be a finite family of pairwise disjoint intervals and
suppose
\[
 \supp b_Q\subset Q,\qquad \int b_Q=0,
 \qquad \|b_Q\|_1\le\Lambda|Q|.
\]
For $s\ge5$, pair $H_j$ with the intervals of length $2^{j-s}$.  Then for
arbitrary fixed coefficients $|\epsilon_Q|\le1$,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:allq-fullkernel-1d}
 \left\|\sum_jH_j*\!\sum_{|Q|=2^{j-s}}\epsilon_Qb_Q\right\|_2^2
 \le C M^2 2^{-s}\Lambda\sum_Q\|b_Q\|_1.
\end{equation}
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
We prove the stronger squared-energy bound with $600\,2^{-2s}$.
Put $\delta=2^{-s}$, and for each packet set
$R_Q=2^{j(Q)}$, where $|Q|=\delta R_Q$; let $c_Q$ be its center.
Since $H_j'$ is continuous, its essential supremum bound is a bound
at every point.  Mean-zero cancellation and the fundamental theorem
of calculus consequently give
\[
 H_{j(Q)}*b_Q(x)=\int_Q
 [H_{j(Q)}(x-y)-H_{j(Q)}(x-c_Q)]b_Q(y)\,dy,
 \qquad
 |H_{j(Q)}(x-y)-H_{j(Q)}(x-c_Q)|\le M\delta/R_Q.
\]
Here $|y-c_Q|\le\delta R_Q$ is sufficient.  If $|x-y|>5R_Q$,
then $|x-c_Q|>4R_Q$, because $\delta\le1$; both kernel values
vanish by the outer support assumption.  Define the positive envelope
\[
 k_Q(z)=\frac{M\delta}{R_Q}\mathbf1_{\{|z|\le5R_Q\}},
 \qquad v_Q=k_Q*|b_Q|.
\]
Thus $|\epsilon_QH_{j(Q)}*b_Q|\le v_Q$ almost everywhere and
$\|k_Q\|_1=10M\delta$.

We spell out the multiscale packing estimate, so that no interaction
budget is being assumed.  Order packets by $R_Q$.  If $R_{Q'}\le R_Q$,
the positive correlation $k_Q*\widetilde k_{Q'}$ is supported in
$[-10R_Q,10R_Q]$ and is bounded by
$(M\delta/R_Q)(10M\delta)$.
For fixed $y$, only intervals $Q'$ meeting
$[y-10R_Q,y+10R_Q]$ can contribute to its convolution with $|b_{Q'}|$.
Such intervals have length at most $R_Q$, and hence are contained in
$[y-15R_Q,y+15R_Q]$.  Their disjoint interiors and the density hypothesis
therefore give, simultaneously across all these scales,
\[
 \sum_{R_{Q'}\le R_Q}\int
 (k_Q*\widetilde k_{Q'})(y-z)|b_{Q'}(z)|\,dz
 \le (10M\delta)(30M\delta\Lambda).
\]
Tonelli now expands the square of $\sum_Qv_Q$ into nonnegative
pairings.  Split the ordered pairs according to which radius is larger
and charge the diagonal at most twice.  Integrating the last bound
against $|b_Q(y)|$ proves
\[
 \int\left(\sum_Qv_Q\right)^2
 \le2(10M\delta)(30M\delta\Lambda)\sum_Q\|b_Q\|_1
 =600M^2\delta^2\Lambda\sum_Q\|b_Q\|_1.
\]
The family is finite, so linearity of the genuine $L^1$ convolutions
regroups the packet sum as the displayed sum over $j$ in the statement.
Pointwise domination by $\sum_Qv_Q$, followed by
$\delta^2\le\delta=2^{-s}$, proves
\eqref{eq:allq-fullkernel-1d}.  Oddness, the inner support bound, and
the second-derivative bound are compatible with this argument but are
not needed for this stronger estimate.

The same reasoning also justifies the countable grouping used when
exhausting a decomposition.  If $\sum_Q\|b_Q\|_1<\infty$, then
$\sum_Q\|H_{j(Q)}*(\epsilon_Qb_Q)\|_1\le M\sum_Q\|b_Q\|_1$.
Tonelli gives absolute summability almost everywhere and permits both
same-scale packet summation inside convolution and subsequent summation
over scales, on one common conull set.  Fatou transfers the stronger
energy bound to that actual output.  When the total mass is infinite
and $M\Lambda>0$, the extended energy bound has infinite right-hand
side, with no assertion of uncontrolled convergence.  If $M=0$,
continuity and the $L^1$ bound force every $H_j$ to be identically zero;
if $\Lambda=0$, every packet vanishes almost everywhere.  Thus the
zero-parameter and empty-family cases are included as well.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Unphased weak endpoint with radial $V^\rho$]
\label{prop:allq-unphased-weak}
For every mean-zero $\Omega\in L\log L(\Sph^{n-1})$ and every bounded
compactly supported $f$, under \eqref{eq:allq-normalize-b},
\begin{equation}\label{eq:allq-unphased-weak}
 \|\mathbf J(T^{0,b}_{\Omega,\varepsilon}f)\|_{L^{1,\infty}}
 \le C_{n,\rho}\mathfrak L(\Omega)\|f\|_1.
\end{equation}
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
We prove the estimate for every $f\in L^1(\R^n)$ and every $n\ge1$.
Initially use the pointwise radial budget
\[
 B(b):=\sup_{r>0}|b(r)|+V^\rho(b;(0,\infty))\le1.
\]
This is exactly the normalization in \eqref{eq:allq-normalize-b}.
Write $F=\|f\|_1$ and $G=\mathfrak L(\Omega)$.  If $G=0$, polar
coordinates give the zero truncation at every positive radius and the
conclusion is immediate.  Suppose $G>0$, fix $\lambda>0$, and perform
the dyadic Calder\'on--Zygmund decomposition at height
$\alpha=\lambda/G$:
\[
 f=g+\sum_Qb_Q,\qquad
 \sum_Q\|b_Q\|_1\le2F,\quad
 \sum_Q|Q|\le F/\alpha,\quad
 \|g\|_2^2\le2^n\alpha F.
\]
Here the sum is the actual $L^1$-convergent sum of the mean-zero bad
functions on the disjoint maximal dyadic cubes; in particular
$\|b_Q\|_1\le2^{n+1}\alpha|Q|$.
Let $\NN$ be the pointwise finite-pair path norm in \eqref{mod:pathnorm}.
Proposition~\ref{prop:allq-terminal-LlogL} at $p=2$, together with
the pointwise comparison $\NN\le2\mathbf J$ in
\eqref{mod:pathequiv}, gives
\[
 |\{\NN(T^{0,b}_{\Omega,\varepsilon}g)>u\}|
 \le C_{n,\rho}G^2\,2^n\alpha F/u^2\qquad(u>0).
\]
Only this strong $L^2$ estimate is used for the good function.

\emph{The original bad functions and the raw angular split.}
For now retain a finite set of bad cubes.  Put
$B_k=\sum_{\ell(Q)=2^k}b_Q$, with this finite restriction understood.
The gap-$s$ path pairs $B_{j-s}$ with the literal shell
$2^j<|z|\le2^{j+1}$, retaining the factor $b(|z|)$ and the positive
cutoff $\varepsilon$.  Fix a structural starting gap $s_0\ge5$ large
enough for Proposition~\ref{prop:allq-terminal-packet}.  Set
\begin{equation}\label{eq:allq-weak-parameters}
 N=4n+1,\quad k_\rho=\lceil\rho\rceil,\quad
 \delta_a=\frac1{6144Nk_\rho},\quad
 h_s=\frac18\,2^{-s/(192N)},\quad
 \Theta_s=\|\Omega\|_1\,2^{\delta_as}.
\end{equation}
Split $\Omega$ into its raw bounded part
$\omega_s=\Omega\mathbf1_{\{|\Omega|\le\Theta_s\}}$ and raw tail
$\Omega_s^\sharp=\Omega\mathbf1_{\{|\Omega|>\Theta_s\}}$.
Neither part is required to have mean zero.  A bounded measurable angular
representative of $\omega_s$ may be used in the kernel estimates:
polar coordinates and absolute convolution identify the resulting
paths on one common conull set for all the gaps and positive radii.
No radial representative is changed.
The raw angular-tail summation and the absolute-convolution $V^1$
envelope give, uniformly in every finite set $\mathcal I$ of retained
gaps,
\[
 \left\|V^1\left(\sum_{s\in\mathcal I}
       \operatorname{Tail}_{s,\varepsilon}^{b}:
       \varepsilon>0\right)\right\|_1
 \le C_{n,\delta_a}GF.
\]
This estimate uses only $|b|\le1$ and the original angular $L\log L$
gauge, not cancellation of either split piece.

\emph{The three majorants for a bounded angular piece.}
Let $U_{s,\varepsilon}^b$ be its actual gap-$s$ continuum path, and put
\[
 \mathcal S_s=
 \left(\sum_j V^2(U_{s,\varepsilon}^b:
                 2^j\le\varepsilon\le2^{j+1})^2\right)^{1/2}.
\]
The unique shell assignment of each bad cube, followed by the full
terminal estimate of Proposition~\ref{prop:allq-terminal-packet}, gives
\begin{equation}\label{eq:allq-weak-short-decay}
 \|\mathcal S_s\|_2^2
 \le C_{n,\rho}\Theta_s^2
     2^{-\delta_{T,\rho}s}\alpha F,
 \qquad
 \delta_{T,\rho}=\min\left\{\frac1{128N},\frac1{64N\rho}\right\}.
\end{equation}
This is the original terminal decay.  For scalar summation only we use
$\delta_{T,\rho}\ge1/(768Nk_\rho)$, so
\[
 \|\mathcal S_s\|_2
 \le C_{n,\rho}\|\Omega\|_1
        2^{-s/(2048Nk_\rho)}(\alpha F)^{1/2}.
\]

Smooth the entire profile
$a_j(u)=b(2^ju)\mathbf1_{(1,2]}(u)$ at width $h_s$, as in
Lemma~\ref{lem:allq-whole-profile-smoothing}, and write
\[
 H_j^{s,b}(z)=K_{\omega_s}(z)(a_j*\phi_{h_s})(2^{-j}|z|),
 \qquad
 \widetilde U_s^b(m)=\sum_{j\ge m}H_j^{s,b}*B_{j-s}.
\]
Define the literal discrete long and error majorants by
\[
 \mathcal L_s=V^2(\widetilde U_s^b(m):m\in\mathbb Z),\qquad
 \mathcal E_s=V^1(U_{s,2^m}^b-\widetilde U_s^b(m):m\in\mathbb Z).
\]
The deterministic dyadic long--short inequality yields pointwise
\[
 V^2(U_{s,\varepsilon}^b:\varepsilon>0)
 \le3(\mathcal S_s+\mathcal L_s+\mathcal E_s).
\]
These three majorants have measurable representatives.  The sharp--smooth
error is a difference of \emph{discrete suffix paths}.  Its $V^1$ norm
is at most twice the sum of the absolute shell errors, hence Young's
inequality and the unique shell assignment give
\begin{equation}\label{eq:allq-weak-unsmooth-sum}
 \|\mathcal E_s\|_1
 \le C_{n,\rho}\Theta_s h_s^{1/\rho}F
 \le C_{n,\rho}\|\Omega\|_1
       2^{-31s/(6144Nk_\rho)}F.
\end{equation}
In particular this is not a radius-truncated thin-boundary estimate.
The last exponent follows from
$1/(192N\rho)-\delta_a\ge31/(6144Nk_\rho)>0$.

The smoothed full-kernel source has normalized radial derivative budget
$M_N\le C_{n,N}h_s^{-N}\Theta_s$, uniformly in $j$.
Apply Lemma~\ref{lem:fullkernel} to the actual original bad-cube packets
in each translated dyadic grid, with fixed coefficients chosen before
the output point.  Truncate the ranks at $2^{(n+1)s}$; the rank-overlap
exception $X_s$ satisfies
\[
 |X_s|\le2^{6n-s}F/\alpha.
\]
The finite translated-grid rank decomposition and the binary fixed-sign
Rademacher--Menshov inequality give the explicit norm factor
$D_{n,s}=2^n((n+1)s+2)\sqrt8$.  Consequently
\begin{equation}\label{eq:allq-weak-long-decay}
 \begin{split}
 \|\mathbf1_{X_s^c}\mathcal L_s\|_2
 &\le D_{n,s}
   \bigl(C_n(h_s^{-N}\Theta_s)^2 2^{-s/24}\alpha F\bigr)^{1/2}\\
 &\le C_{n,\rho}(s+2)\|\Omega\|_1
       2^{-(1/64-\delta_a)s}(\alpha F)^{1/2}.
 \end{split}
\end{equation}
Indeed $h_s^{-N}=8^N2^{s/192}$, so the square-root source decay
$2^{-s/48}$ leaves $2^{-s/64}$ before the angular growth is included.
The exponent $1/64-\delta_a$ is positive.  This argument applies in
every positive dimension and does not require
Lemma~\ref{lem:allq-fullkernel-1d}, a sharper one-dimensional estimate,
or an output-dependent choice of signs.

\emph{Finite-gap distribution and the two exhaustions.}
For a finite gap set $\mathcal I\subset\{s:s\ge s_0\}$ let
$X=\bigcup_{s\in\mathcal I}X_s$.  Sum the actual $L^2$ majorants by
Minkowski on $X^c$ and sum the error and raw-tail envelopes in $L^1$.
The four scalar series for exceptional volume, short variation, long
variation, and smoothing error are finite by the displayed exponents.
Thus there are nonnegative measurable majorants $\mathcal A,\mathcal B$
such that, almost everywhere,
\[
 V^2\left(\sum_{s\in\mathcal I}U_{s,\varepsilon}^{\rm full}:
              \varepsilon>0\right)\le\mathcal A+\mathcal B,
 \quad
 \|\mathcal A\|_1\le C_1GF,\quad
 \|\mathbf1_{X^c}\mathcal B\|_2\le\sqrt{C_2}G(\alpha F)^{1/2},
 \quad |X|\le C_0F/\alpha.
\]
Here $U_s^{\rm full}$ includes both raw angular pieces.  Chebyshev
therefore gives, for $u>0$, constants independent of both the finite
packet set and the finite gap set,
\[
 \left|\left\{V^2\left(\sum_{s\in\mathcal I}
        U_{s,\varepsilon}^{\rm full}\right)>u\right\}\right|
 \le C_0F/\alpha+C_1GF/(u/2)+C_2G^2\alpha F/(u/2)^2.
\]
This sums strong majorants, not weak-$L^1$ quasi-norms or a sequence of
per-gap level sets.

There is one enlarged original Calder\'on--Zygmund set $E_0$, independent
of the finite cutoffs, with
$|E_0|\le2^{n(s_0+3)}F/\alpha$.  Outside $E_0$ every prohibited
near-gap cell vanishes at every radius.  Fix the finite packet set
first and exhaust the gaps $s\ge s_0$.  Literal dyadic-cell exhaustion
and absolute cutoff integrability give convergence to its original
truncation on one common conull set, simultaneously for every positive
radius.  Every strict superlevel witness for $V^2$ uses finitely many
radii, so it persists under this convergence.  The resulting lower
semicontinuity of distribution sets, with the payment
for $E_0$ absorbed into $C_0$, gives
\[
 |\{V^2(T^{0,b}_{\Omega,\varepsilon}b_{\mathcal Q})>u\}|
 \le C_0F/\alpha+C_1GF/(u/2)+C_2G^2\alpha F/(u/2)^2
\]
for every finite original bad-cube sum $b_{\mathcal Q}$.

Now $\NN(H+K)\le\NN(H)+2V^2(K)$, so the good estimate at level
$\lambda/2$ and this bad estimate at level $\lambda/4$, with
$\alpha=\lambda/G$, imply
\[
 |\{\NN(T^{0,b}_{\Omega,\varepsilon}g+
              T^{0,b}_{\Omega,\varepsilon}b_{\mathcal Q})>\lambda\}|
 \le C_{n,\rho}GF/\lambda.
\]
Finally exhaust the original bad cubes.  Their actual $L^1$ series
converges to $f-g$; common all-radius absolute cutoff integrability
gives convergence of these original truncations to
$T^{0,b}_{\Omega,\varepsilon}f$ on a single conull set for every
$\varepsilon>0$.  Strict finite-pair witnesses for $\NN$ pass the last distribution
estimate to the full path by the same lower-semicontinuity argument,
which is valid for outer measure and assumes no level-set measurability.  No $L^2$
approximation of $f$, finite parameter restriction, or uncountable
intersection of exceptional sets is used.  This establishes the
pointwise-inside bound, since $\mathbf J\le\NN$.

The same argument also gives a bound under the weaker essential-supremum
budget. Put
$B_{\rm ess}(b)=\|b\|_{L^\infty(0,\infty)}+V^\rho(b;(0,\infty))$.
For each $r>0$, comparison with an essential-norm anchor gives
\[
 |b(r)|\le\|b\|_{L^\infty(0,\infty)}+V^\rho(b;(0,\infty)),
 \qquad B(b)\le2B_{\rm ess}(b).
\]
Indeed take an anchor outside the essential exceptional set with value
at most the essential supremum plus any prescribed positive error,
apply the two-point variation bound, and let that error tend to zero.
If $B_{\rm ess}(b)=0$, this also proves $b(r)=0$ at every positive
radius.  Otherwise exact scalar normalization by the finite pointwise
budget and homogeneity of the literal truncations restore a factor
at most $2B_{\rm ess}(b)$. In particular, since
$B_{\rm ess}(b)\le B(b)$, the asserted estimate follows under
\eqref{eq:allq-normalize-b}, throughout $1\le\rho<\infty$.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Polynomial weak endpoint with radial $V^\rho$]
\label{prop:allq-polynomial-weak}
Under \eqref{eq:allq-normalize-b}, for every polynomial $P$ of degree at
most $d$, every mean-zero $\Omega\in L\log L(\Sph^{n-1})$, and every
bounded compactly supported $f$,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:allq-polynomial-weak}
 \|\mathbf J(T^{P,b}_{\Omega,\varepsilon}f)\|_{L^{1,\infty}}
 \le C_{n,d,\rho}\mathfrak L(\Omega)\|f\|_1.
\end{equation}
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
If $\mathfrak L(\Omega)=0$, then $\Omega=0$ and the assertion is
immediate. By angular homogeneity assume throughout the induction that
$\mathfrak L(\Omega)=1$.  At each inductive stage impose finite cube and
shell cutoffs and fix a finite rational truncation set $I$; every bound
below is uniform in these choices.  We induct on the largest total degree $m$
carrying a nonpure homogeneous class. If no such class exists, write
$P(x,y)=p(x)+q(y)$ and use
Proposition~\ref{prop:allq-unphased-weak} after input and output
modulation.

Suppose the result is known through nonpure degree $m-1$. Let $P_m$ be
the highest nonpure homogeneous block and put
\[
 C_m=\|P_m\|_{\rm coeff},\qquad r_0=C_m^{-1/m}.
\]
The exact conjugacy
\begin{equation}\label{eq:allq-weak-dilation-conjugacy}
 T^{P,b}_{\Omega,r_0\varepsilon}f(r_0X)
 =T^{\widetilde P,\widetilde b}_{\Omega,\varepsilon}f_{r_0}(X),
 \qquad
 \widetilde b(u)=b(r_0u),
\end{equation}
and \eqref{eq:allq-dilation-b} reduce the proof to
$\|P_m\|_{\rm coeff}=1$ without changing the normalized radial bound.

We now make the far-field parameter choice explicit. Put
\begin{equation}\label{eq:allq-polynomial-weak-parameters}
 d_*=\max(d,1),\qquad
 \kappa_0=\frac1{100d_*},\qquad
 c_{H,\rho}=c_{\kappa_0,\rho},
\end{equation}
where $c_{\kappa_0,\rho}>0$ is given by
\eqref{eq:allq-collective-explicit-choice}, and choose
\begin{equation}\label{eq:allq-polynomial-angular-choice}
 0<\delta_a<
 \frac18\min\{c_{P,\rho},c_{E,\rho},c_{H,\rho}\}.
\end{equation}
Perform the phase-adapted Calder\'on--Zygmund decomposition and write
\[
 b_{\rm CZ}=\sum_Qb_Q,\qquad
 \Sigma=\sum_Q\|b_Q\|_1\le C\|f\|_1.
\]
The good part is controlled by \eqref{eq:allq-strong-core}: since the
Calder\'on--Zygmund decomposition gives
$\|g\|_2^2\le C\alpha\|f\|_1$, the finite-path set
\[
 E_{\rm good}^P
